rray timeline

Testing a combined plan and iterator struct, and why it was slower

Codex · Sep 13, 2026 · 14 prompts · 46 replies · #63

Sep 13, 2026, 19:17 UTC

Davis

So I’m pretty happy with the work in this pr to get us off macros

But I’m still not quite satisfied with the fact that we have 2 structs (plan and iterator)

We’ve discussed how the iterator holds mutable state and the other is immutable and somehow that helps the compiler keep the mutable state in registers. But how? Why? Is that really the correct reasoning? Can we do with just one without loss of performance?

Codex

I’ll trace the current plan and iterator definitions, then inspect the generated C and benchmark setup if one exists. The key distinction to verify is whether the split itself helps register allocation, or whether smaller functions, aliasing, and inlining are doing the real work.

//
// - Shared leading dimensions of size 1. Adding [1, 1, 4] to [1, 3, 4]
//   absorbs the shared first axis and produces dimensions [3, 4], with first
//   input strides [0, 1] and second input strides [1, 3].
//
// - Reducing over the first axis. Reducing a [2, 3, 4] array to [1, 3, 4]
//   produces output strides [0, 1, 3], which coalesce to dimensions [2, 12]
//   with output strides [0, 1]. Each inner run accumulates into one fixed
//   output location.
struct rray_strided_iterator_plan {
  r_ssize size;
  r_ssize v_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  int dimensionality;
  r_ssize v_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
};

struct rray_strided_iterator {
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* plan;
  r_ssize run_start;
  r_ssize location;
  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
};

static inline struct rray_strided_iterator_plan rray_strided_iterator_plan(
  const int* v_dimensions,
  int dimensionality,
  const r_ssize* v_strides
) {
  check_max_dimensionality(dimensionality);

  struct rray_strided_iterator_plan plan;

  plan.size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);

  for (int i = 0; i < dimensionality; ++i) {
    plan.v_dimensions[i] = (r_ssize) v_dimensions[i];
    plan.v_strides[i] = v_strides[i];
  }
  plan.dimensionality = rray__strided_iterator_axes_coalesce(
    plan.v_dimensions,
    plan.v_strides,
    dimensionality
  );

  return plan;
}

// Strided iterator specific to broadcasting
//
// An axis of `from` with a dimension of 1 gets a stride of 0, so it stands
// still while the matching axis of `to` walks. Axes past
// `from_dimensionality` are treated as dimension 1.
static inline struct rray_strided_iterator_plan rray_broadcast_iterator_plan(
  const int* v_from_dimensions,
  int from_dimensionality,
  const int* v_to_dimensions,
  int to_dimensionality
) {
  check_max_dimensionality(to_dimensionality);

  rray__check_broadcast_dimensions(
    v_from_dimensions,
    from_dimensionality,
    v_to_dimensions,
    to_dimensionality
  );

  r_ssize v_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];

  rray__fill_broadcast_strides(
    v_from_dimensions,
    from_dimensionality,
    to_dimensionality,
    v_strides
  );

  return rray_strided_iterator_plan(
    v_to_dimensions,
    to_dimensionality,
    v_strides
  );
}

static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_size(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* plan
) {
  return plan->v_dimensions[0];
}
static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_stride(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* plan
) {
  return plan->v_strides[0];
}

static inline struct rray_strided_iterator rray_strided_iterator(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* plan
) {
  struct rray_strided_iterator it;
  it.plan = plan;
  it.run_start = 0;
  it.location = 0;
  memset(it.v_point, 0, sizeof(it.v_point));
  return it;
}

static inline bool rray_strided_iterator_finished(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator* it
) {
  return it->run_start == it->plan->size;
}

static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_run_start(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator* it
) {
  return it->run_start;
}
static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_location(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator* it
) {
  return it->location;
}
static inline void rray_strided_iterator_next(
  struct rray_strided_iterator* it
) {
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* plan = it->plan;
  it->run_start += rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_size(plan);
  for (int axis = 1; axis < plan->dimensionality; ++axis) {
    ++it->v_point[axis];
    if (it->v_point[axis] < plan->v_dimensions[axis]) {
      it->location += plan->v_strides[axis];
      return;
    }
    it->v_point[axis] = 0;
    it->location -= (plan->v_dimensions[axis] - 1) * plan->v_strides[axis];
  }
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------

// Same as `rray_strided_iterator`, but reports in two location spaces while
// only walking the point space once
struct rray_strided_iterator2_plan {
  r_ssize size;
  r_ssize v_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  int dimensionality;
  r_ssize v_strides1[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  r_ssize v_strides2[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
};

struct rray_strided_iterator2 {
  const struct rray_strided_iterator2_plan* plan;
  r_ssize run_start;
  r_ssize location1;
  r_ssize location2;
  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
};

static inline struct rray_strided_iterator2_plan rray_strided_iterator2_plan(
  const int* v_dimensions,
  int dimensionality,
  const r_ssize* v_strides1,
  const r_ssize* v_strides2
) {
  check_max_dimensionality(dimensionality);

  struct rray_strided_iterator2_plan plan;

  plan.size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);

  for (int i = 0; i < dimensionality; ++i) {
    plan.v_dimensions[i] = (r_ssize) v_dimensions[i];
    plan.v_strides1[i] = v_strides1[i];
    plan.v_strides2[i] = v_strides2[i];
  }
  plan.dimensionality = rray__strided_iterator_axes_coalesce2(
    plan.v_dimensions,
    plan.v_strides1,
    plan.v_strides2,
    dimensionality
  );

  return plan;
}

// one shared `to` space
static inline struct rray_strided_iterator2_plan rray_broadcast_iterator2_plan(
  const int* v_from1_dimensions,
  int from1_dimensionality,
  const int* v_from2_dimensions,

  const int* v_to_dimensions,

# Replace strided iteration macros with a run cursor

Not built. This document records a working experiment in this branch and the
rules needed to turn it into a mergeable feature.

The experiment removes `RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR_FOR_EACH()` and
`RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR2_FOR_EACH()` from `src/strided-iterator.h`. It replaces
them with a plan plus cursor API. All existing callers were ported and the test
suite passes. The current working tree is deliberately left uncommitted so a
future agent can inspect, revise, or discard it.

The central constraint is performance. A natural element-at-a-time iterator
regresses because it performs row-boundary work for every element. The API must
therefore expose first-axis runs. Callers own the outer loop and retain a small,
simple inner loop.

## Goal

Replace the strided iteration macros with explicit state and functions that
allow callers to control traversal. Callers should be able to stop early,
choose their own inner loop, nest iteration, and make the iteration state clear
at the call site.

The API must retain the existing properties:

- coalesce compatible axes before traversing them;
- process the coalesced first axis as one run;
- keep point carry work outside the element loop;
- keep zero first-axis strides visible as fixed paths in caller code;
- support one and two mapped location spaces;
- stay within normal benchmark noise for the present macro implementation.

## The API

An iterator is an immutable traversal plan. A cursor is the mutable state for
one pass over that plan. Separating them is important. A caller commonly
receives a pointer to a plan, then creates a stack-local cursor. Clang can keep
the cursor's changing fields in registers much more readily than fields stored
through the plan pointer.

```c
struct rray_strided_iterator {
  r_ssize size;
  r_ssize v_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  int dimensionality;
  r_ssize v_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
};

struct rray_strided_iterator_cursor {
  const struct rray_strided_iterator* iterator;
  r_ssize index;
  r_ssize location;
  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
};

struct rray_strided_iterator2 {
  r_ssize size;
  r_ssize v_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  int dimensionality;
  r_ssize v_strides1[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  r_ssize v_strides2[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
};

struct rray_strided_iterator2_cursor {
  const struct rray_strided_iterator2* iterator;
  r_ssize index;
  r_ssize location1;
  r_ssize location2;
  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
};
```

Keep the existing constructors and coalescing logic:

```c
struct rray_strided_iterator rray_strided_iterator(...);
struct rray_strided_iterator rray_broadcast_iterator(...);

struct rray_strided_iterator2 rray_strided_iterator2(...);
struct rray_strided_iterator2 rray_broadcast_iterator2(...);
```

Add these `static inline` functions for one mapped location:

```c
struct rray_strided_iterator_cursor rray_strided_iterator_begin(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator* iterator
);

bool rray_strided_iterator_finished(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_cursor* cursor
);

r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_index(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_cursor* cursor
);

r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_location(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_cursor* cursor
);

r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_run_size(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_cursor* cursor
);

r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_run_stride(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_cursor* cursor
);

void rray_strided_iterator_next(
  struct rray_strided_iterator_cursor* cursor
);
```

The two-location form has the same shape:

```c
struct rray_strided_iterator2_cursor rray_strided_iterator2_begin(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator2* iterator
);

bool rray_strided_iterator2_finished(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator2_cursor* cursor
);

r_ssize rray_strided_iterator2_index(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator2_cursor* cursor
);

r_ssize rray_strided_iterator2_location1(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator2_cursor* cursor
);

r_ssize rray_strided_iterator2_location2(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator2_cursor* cursor
);

r_ssize rray_strided_iterator2_run_size(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator2_cursor* cursor
);

r_ssize rray_strided_iterator2_run_stride1(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator2_cursor* cursor
);

r_ssize rray_strided_iterator2_run_stride2(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator2_cursor* cursor
);

void rray_strided_iterator2_next(
  struct rray_strided_iterator2_cursor* cursor
);
```

`index()` and each `location()` report the first element of the current run.
`run_size()` is the coalesced first-axis dimension. `next()` advances through
the entire current run and positions the cursor at the next one. It does not
step one element.

This is intentional. An element-wise `next()` would have to test for the end
of a run and possibly carry into later axes on every element. The existing
macro avoids exactly that work.

## Cursor behaviour

`begin()` sets `index` and every location to zero, clears `v_point`, and stores
the plan pointer. `finished()` is true when `index == iterator->size`.

`next()` must do this in order:

1. Add `run_size()` to `cursor->index`.
2. Return if the new index equals the plan size.
3. Starting at axis 1, increment the point coordinate.
4. If the coordinate is still within that axis dimension, add that axis stride
   to each location and return.
5. Otherwise reset the coordinate to zero, subtract the completed extent times
   each stride, and continue to the next axis.

Do not subtract `run_size() * run_stride()` inside `next()`. The cursor stores
the beginning of the current run. The caller increments a local location in
- reductions over the first axis;
- `rray_split()` layouts where either output-list or output-element location is
  fixed across a run.

## Operation dispatch must stay outside every inner loop

This is the most important lesson from the experiment.

`equal.c` originally chose equal versus not-equal before traversal. `extremum.c`
originally chose missing-value propagation versus removal before traversal.
The first port used conditional expressions inside every element loop to avoid
duplicating the four stride paths. That was wrong.

The dedicated benchmark results from that version were:

- equality and inequality: 34 to 35% slower;

The likely advantage is that the mutable object is created inside the hot worker function, so its address does not escape that function and Clang can replace its scalar fields with local SSA values. //
// - Shared leading dimensions of size 1. Adding [1, 1, 4] to [1, 3, 4]
//   absorbs the shared first axis and produces dimensions [3, 4], with first
//   input strides [0, 1] and second input strides [1, 3].
//
// - Reducing over the first axis. Reducing a [2, 3, 4] array to [1, 3, 4]
//   produces output strides [0, 1, 3], which coalesce to dimensions [2, 12]
//   with output strides [0, 1]. Each inner run accumulates into one fixed
//   output location.
struct rray_strided_iterator {
  r_ssize index;
  r_ssize size;

  // Since coalescing can multiply two axes' dimensions together, we use an
  // `r_ssize` here even though an individual dimension can't be above an `int`.
  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  r_ssize v_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  int dimensionality;

  r_ssize location;
  r_ssize v_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
};

static inline struct rray_strided_iterator rray_strided_iterator(
  const int* v_dimensions,
  int dimensionality,
  const r_ssize* v_strides
) {
  check_max_dimensionality(dimensionality);

  struct rray_strided_iterator it;

  it.index = 0;
  it.size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);

  for (int i = 0; i < dimensionality; ++i) {
    it.v_dimensions[i] = (r_ssize) v_dimensions[i];
    it.v_strides[i] = v_strides[i];
  }
  memset(it.v_point, 0, sizeof(r_ssize) * dimensionality);
  it.location = 0;

  it.dimensionality = rray__strided_iterator_axes_coalesce(
    it.v_dimensions,
    it.v_strides,
    dimensionality
  );

  return it;
}

// Strided iterator specific to broadcasting
//
// An axis of `from` with a dimension of 1 gets a stride of 0, so it stands
// still while the matching axis of `to` walks. Axes past
// `from_dimensionality` are treated as dimension 1.
static inline struct rray_strided_iterator rray_broadcast_iterator(
  const int* v_from_dimensions,
  int from_dimensionality,
  const int* v_to_dimensions,
  int to_dimensionality
) {
  check_max_dimensionality(to_dimensionality);

  rray__check_broadcast_dimensions(
    v_from_dimensions,
    from_dimensionality,
    v_to_dimensions,
    to_dimensionality
  );

  r_ssize v_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];

  rray__fill_broadcast_strides(
    v_from_dimensions,
    from_dimensionality,
    to_dimensionality,
    v_strides
  );

  return rray_strided_iterator(v_to_dimensions, to_dimensionality, v_strides);
}

// For-loop-style iteration, giving access to the flat index and mapped
// location.
//
// The loop structure is more complicated than strictly necessary for
// performance reasons. We process a run along the first axis while holding all
// other axes fixed, so the inner loop only advances the index and location by
// fixed increments. Point adjustment happens between runs. This makes the
// inner loop easier for the compiler to optimize and vectorize. In our
// benchmarks, batching rows improved performance by up to 3-4× and helped our
// operations outperform base R in many cases, even with broadcasting support.
#define RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR_FOR_EACH(IT, INDEX, LOCATION, ...)               \
  do {                                                                         \
    struct rray_strided_iterator* const iterator = (IT);                       \
    const r_ssize* v_strides = iterator->v_strides;                            \
    const r_ssize row_stride = v_strides[0];                                   \
                                                                               \
    if (row_stride == 0) {                                                     \
      RRAY__STRIDED_ITERATOR_FOR_EACH(IT, INDEX, LOCATION, 0, __VA_ARGS__);    \
    } else {                                                                   \
      RRAY__STRIDED_ITERATOR_FOR_EACH(                                         \
        IT,                                                                    \
        INDEX,                                                                 \
        LOCATION,                                                              \
        row_stride,                                                            \
        __VA_ARGS__                                                            \
      );                                                                       \
    }                                                                          \
  } while (0)

#define RRAY__STRIDED_ITERATOR_FOR_EACH(IT, INDEX, LOCATION, ROW_STRIDE, ...)  \
  do {                                                                         \
    /* Unpacking the iterator fields is critical for performance. It gives */  \
    /* the compiler guarantees about loop counters and fixed inputs that */    \
    /* unlock vectorization optimizations. */                                  \
    struct rray_strided_iterator* const iterator = (IT);                       \
    r_ssize INDEX = iterator->index;                                           \
    const r_ssize size = iterator->size;                                       \
    r_ssize* v_point = iterator->v_point;                                      \
    const r_ssize* v_dimensions = iterator->v_dimensions;                      \
    const int dimensionality = iterator->dimensionality;                       \
    r_ssize LOCATION = iterator->location;                                     \
    const r_ssize* v_strides = iterator->v_strides;                            \
                                                                               \
    const r_ssize rows = v_dimensions[0];                                      \
    const r_ssize row_reset = rows * ROW_STRIDE;                               \
                                                                               \
    while (INDEX != size) {                                                    \
      /* Apply expression for each row */                                      \
      for (r_ssize row = 0; row < rows; ++row) {                               \
        __VA_ARGS__                                                            \
        LOCATION += ROW_STRIDE;                                                \
        ++INDEX;                                                               \
      }                                                                        \
      LOCATION -= row_reset;                                                   \
                                                                               \
      /* We've iterated over the rows of this batch. */                        \
      /* Advance to the start of the next batch of rows. */                    \
      for (int axis = 1; axis < dimensionality; ++axis) {                      \
        ++v_point[axis];                                                       \
                                                                               \
        if (v_point[axis] < v_dimensions[axis]) {                              \
          LOCATION += v_strides[axis];                                         \
          break;                                                               \
        }                                                                      \
                                                                               \
        v_point[axis] = 0;                                                     \
        LOCATION -= (v_dimensions[axis] - 1) * v_strides[axis];                \
      }                                                                        \
    }                                                                          \
  } while (0)

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------

#include "arithmetic.h"

#include "broadcast-names.h"
#include "dimensionality.h"
#include "dimensions.h"
#include "size.h"
#include "type.h"
#include "utils.h"

r_obj* rray_binary_arithmetic(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* y,
  rray_binary_arithmetic_switch_fn fn_switch,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct rray_arg* y_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  check_unclassed(x, x_arg, error_call);
  check_unclassed(y, y_arg, error_call);

  x = KEEP(arg_as_array(x, x_arg, error_call));
  y = KEEP(arg_as_array(y, y_arg, error_call));

  const rray_binary_arithmetic_fn fn =
    fn_switch(x, y, x_arg, y_arg, error_call);

  r_obj* x_dimensions = r_dim(x);
  r_obj* y_dimensions = r_dim(y);

  const int* v_x_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(x_dimensions);
  const int* v_y_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(y_dimensions);

  const int x_dimensionality =
    rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(x_dimensions);
  const int y_dimensionality =
    rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(y_dimensions);

  r_obj* dimensions = KEEP(rray_dimensions2(
    v_x_dimensions,
    x_dimensionality,
    v_y_dimensions,
    y_dimensionality,
    x_arg,
    y_arg,
    error_call
  ));
  const int* v_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(dimensions);
  const int dimensionality = rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(dimensions);

  const r_ssize size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);

  struct rray_strided_iterator2 it = rray_broadcast_iterator2(
    v_x_dimensions,
    x_dimensionality,
    v_y_dimensions,
    y_dimensionality,
    v_dimensions,
    dimensionality
  );

  r_obj* out = KEEP(fn(x, y, size, &it, error_call));
  r_attrib_poke_dim(out, dimensions);

  r_obj* out_names = KEEP(rray_broadcast_names2(x, y, dimensions));

  if (out_names != r_null) {
    r_attrib_poke_dim_names(out, out_names);
  }

  FREE(5);
  return out;
}

r_no_return void stop_unsupported_arithmetic(
  const char* op,
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* y,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct rray_arg* y_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  r_abort_lazy_call(
    error_call,
    "Can't apply `%s` to %s and %s.",
    op,
    rray_arg_type_format(x_arg, rray_typeof(x)),
    rray_arg_type_format(y_arg, rray_typeof(y))
  );
}

#define RRAY_ARITHMETIC(                                                       \
  X_CTYPE,                                                                     \
  X_CONST_DEREF,                                                               \
  X_CAST,                                                                      \
  Y_CTYPE,                                                                     \
  Y_CONST_DEREF,                                                               \
  Y_CAST,                                                                      \
  OUT_RTYPE,                                                                   \
  OUT_CTYPE,                                                                   \
  OUT_DEREF,                                                                   \
  ONE                                                                          \
)                                                                              \
  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_vector(OUT_RTYPE, size));                          \
  OUT_CTYPE* v_out = OUT_DEREF(out);                                           \
                                                                               \
  const X_CTYPE* v_x = X_CONST_DEREF(x);                                       \
  const Y_CTYPE* v_y = Y_CONST_DEREF(y);                                       \
                                                                               \
  RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR2_FOR_EACH(it, i, x_loc, y_loc, {                       \
    v_out[i] = ONE(X_CAST(v_x[x_loc]), Y_CAST(v_y[y_loc]), error_call);        \
  });                                                                          \
                                                                               \
  FREE(1);                                                                     \
  return out;

#endif
  case RRAY_TYPE2_integer_scalar:
  case RRAY_TYPE2_double_scalar:
  case RRAY_TYPE2_complex_scalar:
  case RRAY_TYPE2_character_scalar:

}

  );

# Replace strided iteration macros with a run cursor

Not built. This document records a working experiment in this branch and the
rules needed to turn it into a mergeable feature.

The experiment removes `RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR_FOR_EACH()` and
`RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR2_FOR_EACH()` from `src/strided-iterator.h`. It replaces
them with a plan plus cursor API. All existing callers were ported and the test
suite passes. The current working tree is deliberately left uncommitted so a
future agent can inspect, revise, or discard it.

The central constraint is performance. A natural element-at-a-time iterator
regresses because it performs row-boundary work for every element. The API must
therefore expose first-axis runs. Callers own the outer loop and retain a small,
simple inner loop.

## Goal

Replace the strided iteration macros with explicit state and functions that
allow callers to control traversal. Callers should be able to stop early,
choose their own inner loop, nest iteration, and make the iteration state clear

59 more lines cut

   149	  r_ssize location;
   150	  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
   151	};
   152	
   153	static inline struct rray_strided_iterator_plan rray_strided_iterator_plan(
   154	  const int* v_dimensions,
   155	  int dimensionality,
   156	  const r_ssize* v_strides
   157	) {
   158	  check_max_dimensionality(dimensionality);
   159	
   160	  struct rray_strided_iterator_plan plan;
   161	
   162	  plan.size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);
   163	
   164	  for (int i = 0; i < dimensionality; ++i) {
   165	    plan.v_dimensions[i] = (r_ssize) v_dimensions[i];
   166	    plan.v_strides[i] = v_strides[i];
   167	  }
   168	  plan.dimensionality = rray__strided_iterator_axes_coalesce(
   169	    plan.v_dimensions,
   170	    plan.v_strides,
   171	    dimensionality
   172	  );
   173	
   174	  return plan;
   175	}
   176	
   177	// Strided iterator specific to broadcasting
   178	//
   179	// An axis of `from` with a dimension of 1 gets a stride of 0, so it stands
   180	// still while the matching axis of `to` walks. Axes past
   181	// `from_dimensionality` are treated as dimension 1.
   182	static inline struct rray_strided_iterator_plan rray_broadcast_iterator_plan(
   183	  const int* v_from_dimensions,
   184	  int from_dimensionality,
   185	  const int* v_to_dimensions,
   186	  int to_dimensionality
   187	) {
   188	  check_max_dimensionality(to_dimensionality);
   189	
   190	  rray__check_broadcast_dimensions(
   191	    v_from_dimensions,
   192	    from_dimensionality,
   193	    v_to_dimensions,
   194	    to_dimensionality
   195	  );
   196	
   197	  r_ssize v_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
   198	
   199	  rray__fill_broadcast_strides(
   200	    v_from_dimensions,
   201	    from_dimensionality,
   202	    to_dimensionality,
   203	    v_strides
   204	  );
   205	
   206	  return rray_strided_iterator_plan(
   207	    v_to_dimensions,
   208	    to_dimensionality,
   209	    v_strides
   210	  );
   211	}
   212	
   213	static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_size(
   214	  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* plan
   215	) {
   216	  return plan->v_dimensions[0];
   217	}
   218	static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_stride(
   219	  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* plan
   220	) {
   221	  return plan->v_strides[0];
   222	}
   223	
   224	static inline struct rray_strided_iterator rray_strided_iterator(
   225	  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* plan
   226	) {
   227	  struct rray_strided_iterator it;
   228	  it.plan = plan;
   229	  it.run_start = 0;
   230	  it.location = 0;
   231	  memset(it.v_point, 0, sizeof(it.v_point));
   232	  return it;
   233	}
   234	
   235	static inline bool rray_strided_iterator_finished(
   236	  const struct rray_strided_iterator* it
   237	) {
   238	  return it->run_start == it->plan->size;
   239	}
   240	
   241	static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_run_start(
   242	  const struct rray_strided_iterator* it
   243	) {
   244	  return it->run_start;
   245	}
   246	static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_location(
   247	  const struct rray_strided_iterator* it
   248	) {
   249	  return it->location;
   250	}
   251	static inline void rray_strided_iterator_next(
   252	  struct rray_strided_iterator* it
   253	) {
   254	  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* plan = it->plan;
   255	  it->run_start += rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_size(plan);
   256	  for (int axis = 1; axis < plan->dimensionality; ++axis) {
   257	    ++it->v_point[axis];
   258	    if (it->v_point[axis] < plan->v_dimensions[axis]) {
   259	      it->location += plan->v_strides[axis];
   260	      return;
   261	    }
   262	    it->v_point[axis] = 0;
   263	    it->location -= (plan->v_dimensions[axis] - 1) * plan->v_strides[axis];
   264	  }
   265	}
   266	
   267	// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   268	
   269	// Same as `rray_strided_iterator`, but reports in two location spaces while
   270	// only walking the point space once
   271	struct rray_strided_iterator2_plan {
   272	  r_ssize size;
   273	  r_ssize v_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
   274	  int dimensionality;
   275	  r_ssize v_strides1[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
   276	  r_ssize v_strides2[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
   277	};
   278	
   279	struct rray_strided_iterator2 {
   280	  const struct rray_strided_iterator2_plan* plan;
   281	  r_ssize run_start;
   282	  r_ssize location1;
   283	  r_ssize location2;
   284	  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
   285	};
   286	
   287	static inline struct rray_strided_iterator2_plan rray_strided_iterator2_plan(
   288	  const int* v_dimensions,
   289	  int dimensionality,
   290	  const r_ssize* v_strides1,
   291	  const r_ssize* v_strides2
   292	) {
   293	  check_max_dimensionality(dimensionality);
   294	
   295	  struct rray_strided_iterator2_plan plan;
   296	
   297	  plan.size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);
   298	
   299	  for (int i = 0; i < dimensionality; ++i) {
   300	    plan.v_dimensions[i] = (r_ssize) v_dimensions[i];
   301	    plan.v_strides1[i] = v_strides1[i];
   302	    plan.v_strides2[i] = v_strides2[i];
   303	  }
   304	  plan.dimensionality = rray__strided_iterator_axes_coalesce2(
   305	    plan.v_dimensions,
   306	    plan.v_strides1,
   307	    plan.v_strides2,
   308	    dimensionality
   309	  );
   310	
   311	  return plan;
   312	}
   313	
   314	// Same as `rray_broadcast_iterator()`, but broadcasts two `from` spaces into
   315	// one shared `to` space
   316	static inline struct rray_strided_iterator2_plan rray_broadcast_iterator2_plan(
   317	  const int* v_from1_dimensions,
   318	  int from1_dimensionality,
   319	  const int* v_from2_dimensions,
   320	  int from2_dimensionality,
   321	  const int* v_to_dimensions,
   322	  int to_dimensionality
   323	) {
   324	  check_max_dimensionality(to_dimensionality);
   325	
   326	  rray__check_broadcast_dimensions(
   327	    v_from1_dimensions,
   328	    from1_dimensionality,
   329	    v_to_dimensions,
   330	    to_dimensionality
   331	  );
   332	
   333	  rray__check_broadcast_dimensions(
   334	    v_from2_dimensions,

Outlining one-struct iteration refactor and vectorization evidence

Bash: git status --short 
$ git status --short
Script completed
Wall time 0.2 seconds
Output:

The current reasoning is too strong. Splitting the plan from the iterator does not, by itself, put mutable state in registers.

What actually helps Clang is:

  • The iterator is local to the worker function.
  • Its address does not escape after the inline helpers are expanded.
  • run_start, location1, and location2 are simple loop-carried values.
  • The large, dynamically indexed v_point array stays on the stack.

Clang first replaces eligible struct fields with scalar SSA values. Register allocation happens later. The source-level const on the plan is not the important part. It prevents writes through that pointer, but it does not prove that another alias cannot change the object.

That is what happens in the current code. The plan crosses the function-pointer boundary in arithmetic.c, while the mutable iterator is created inside the worker in arithmetic.h. In the generated ARM64 code for double addition:

  • run_start is held in x28.
  • The two locations are held in x12 and x11.
  • The point array remains in the worker’s stack frame.
  • Plan values such as run size and first-axis strides are loaded once before traversal.

So the split is helping by creating a non-escaping local object, not because immutable and mutable fields must have different struct types.

There is also strong evidence that one struct can work. I compiled the main implementation, where the plan and iteration state are in one struct. Its generated code keeps the index and both locations in registers and vectorizes the double loop. In fact, that worker has a 0x70 byte stack frame, versus 0x260 for the current split implementation. The old macro explicitly unpacked the scalar fields, so this does not prove that a macro-free merged version will be identical, but it does disprove the idea that the struct split is required for register allocation.

I would try one combined struct:

struct rray_strided_iterator2 {
  r_ssize size;
  r_ssize v_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  int dimensionality;
  r_ssize v_strides1[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  r_ssize v_strides2[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];

  r_ssize run_start;
  r_ssize location1;
  r_ssize location2;
  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
};

The constructor would build the traversal information and initialize the state. Workers would receive a mutable pointer and use the same inline accessors and next() function.

The one real risk is the function boundary. The combined object would be created by the caller and passed through a function pointer. Because the mutable state is then caller-owned rather than a visibly local object, Clang has weaker aliasing information. It may still keep the scalars in registers, but that needs verification. In particular, check operations containing possible error calls, not just double addition.

The semantic argument for two structs is also weak here. Separate plans are useful when you reuse one plan, restart it, or run two cursors over it. The current callers create exactly one iterator for each plan. None currently use that capability.

My conclusion:

  • The explanation in no-macros.md should not say the split itself is better for register allocation.
  • One struct is likely possible without a performance loss.
  • It is not proven until we compile and benchmark the macro-free combined version.
  • Given the simpler model and lack of plan reuse, I would prefer one struct if its assembly retains scalar loop state in registers and the benchmark matrix stays flat.

r_obj* ffi_rray_broadcast(
  r_obj* ffi_x,
  r_obj* ffi_dimensions,
  r_obj* ffi_frame
) {
  struct r_lazy error_call = {.x = ffi_frame, .env = r_null};
  return rray_broadcast(ffi_x, ffi_dimensions, rray_args.x, error_call);
}

r_obj* rray_broadcast(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* dimensions,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  check_unclassed(x, arg, error_call);
  x = KEEP(arg_as_array(x, arg, error_call));

  dimensions =
    KEEP(arg_as_dimensions(dimensions, rray_args.dimensions, error_call));

  r_obj* x_dimensions = KEEP(rray_dimensions(x, arg, error_call));

  const int* v_x_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(x_dimensions);
  const int* v_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(dimensions);

  const int x_dimensionality =
    rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(x_dimensions);
  const int dimensionality = rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(dimensions);

  if (
    rray_dimensions_are_equal(
      v_x_dimensions,
      x_dimensionality,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality
    )
  ) {
    FREE(3);
    return x;
  }

  check_broadcastable(
    v_x_dimensions,
    x_dimensionality,
    v_dimensions,
    dimensionality,
    arg,
    error_call
  );

  const r_ssize size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);

  struct rray_strided_iterator_plan plan = rray_broadcast_iterator_plan(
    v_x_dimensions,
    x_dimensionality,
    v_dimensions,
    dimensionality
  );

  r_obj* out;

  switch (r_typeof(x)) {
  case R_TYPE_logical:
    out = rray_broadcast_lgl(x, size, &plan);
    break;
  case R_TYPE_integer:
    out = rray_broadcast_int(x, size, &plan);
    break;
  case R_TYPE_double:
    out = rray_broadcast_dbl(x, size, &plan);
    break;
  case R_TYPE_complex:
    out = rray_broadcast_cpl(x, size, &plan);
    break;
  case R_TYPE_raw:
    out = rray_broadcast_raw(x, size, &plan);
    break;
  case R_TYPE_character:
    out = rray_broadcast_chr(x, size, &plan);
    break;
  case R_TYPE_list:
    out = rray_broadcast_list(x, size, &plan);
    break;
  default:
    r_stop_unreachable();
  }

  KEEP(out);
  r_attrib_poke_dim(out, dimensions);

  r_obj* out_names = KEEP(rray_broadcast_names(x, dimensions));

  if (out_names != r_null) {
    r_attrib_poke_dim_names(out, out_names);
  }

  FREE(5);
  return out;
}

#define RRAY_BROADCAST_ATOMIC(RTYPE, CTYPE, CONST_DEREF, DEREF)                \
  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_vector(RTYPE, size));                              \
  const CTYPE* v_x = CONST_DEREF(x);                                           \
  CTYPE* v_out = DEREF(out);                                                   \
                                                                               \
  const r_ssize run_size = rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_size(plan);          \
  const r_ssize run_stride = rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_stride(plan);      \
                                                                               \
  for (struct rray_strided_iterator it = rray_strided_iterator(plan);          \
       !rray_strided_iterator_finished(&it);                                   \
       rray_strided_iterator_next(&it)) {                                      \
    const r_ssize run_start = rray_strided_iterator_run_start(&it);            \
    const r_ssize run_end = run_start + run_size;                              \
    r_ssize loc = rray_strided_iterator_location(&it);                         \
                                                                               \
    if (run_stride == 0) {                                                     \
      const CTYPE x_elt = v_x[loc];                                            \
      for (r_ssize i = run_start; i < run_end; ++i) {                          \
        v_out[i] = x_elt;                                                      \
      }                                                                        \
    } else {                                                                   \
      for (r_ssize i = run_start; i < run_end; ++i) {                          \
        v_out[i] = v_x[loc];                                                   \
        loc += run_stride;                                                     \
      }                                                                        \
    }                                                                          \
  }                                                                            \
                                                                               \
  FREE(1);                                                                     \
  return out;

#define RRAY_BROADCAST_BARRIER(RTYPE, CONST_DEREF, POKE)                       \
  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_vector(RTYPE, size));                              \
  r_obj* const* v_x = CONST_DEREF(x);                                          \
                                                                               \
  const r_ssize run_size = rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_size(plan);          \
  const r_ssize run_stride = rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_stride(plan);      \
                                                                               \
  for (struct rray_strided_iterator it = rray_strided_iterator(plan);          \
       !rray_strided_iterator_finished(&it);                                   \
       rray_strided_iterator_next(&it)) {                                      \
    const r_ssize run_start = rray_strided_iterator_run_start(&it);            \
    const r_ssize run_end = run_start + run_size;                              \
    r_ssize loc = rray_strided_iterator_location(&it);                         \
                                                                               \
    if (run_stride == 0) {                                                     \
      r_obj* const x_elt = v_x[loc];                                           \
      for (r_ssize i = run_start; i < run_end; ++i) {                          \
        POKE(out, i, x_elt);                                                   \
      }                                                                        \
    } else {                                                                   \
      for (r_ssize i = run_start; i < run_end; ++i) {                          \
        POKE(out, i, v_x[loc]);                                                \
        loc += run_stride;                                                     \
      }                                                                        \
    }                                                                          \
  }                                                                            \
                                                                               \
  FREE(1);                                                                     \
  return out;

static r_obj* rray_broadcast_lgl(
  r_obj* x,
  r_ssize size,
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* plan
) {
  RRAY_BROADCAST_ATOMIC(R_TYPE_logical, int, r_lgl_cbegin, r_lgl_begin);
}

  RRAY_BROADCAST_ATOMIC(R_TYPE_integer, int, r_int_cbegin, r_int_begin);

Designing new strided iterator header

Thinking

Designing new strided iterator header

Refining v2 iterator naming and initialization

The cleanest POC is a parallel API with _v2 names. That keeps every untouched consumer on the current implementation and makes the performance comparison easy to reverse. Each arity will have one combined struct, constructed once and mutated directly by the worker.

+#ifndef RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR2_H
+#define RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR2_H
+
+#include "strided-iterator.h"
+
+struct rray_strided_iterator_v2 {
+  r_ssize run_start;
+  r_ssize size;
+  r_ssize location;
+  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
+  r_ssize v_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
+  int dimensionality;
+  r_ssize v_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
+};
+
+static inline struct rray_strided_iterator_v2 rray_strided_iterator_v2(
+  const int* v_dimensions,
+  int dimensionality,
+  const r_ssize* v_strides
+) {
+  check_max_dimensionality(dimensionality);
+
+  struct rray_strided_iterator_v2 it;
+
+  it.run_start = 0;
+  it.size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);
+  it.location = 0;
+  memset(it.v_point, 0, sizeof(r_ssize) * dimensionality);
+
+  for (int i = 0; i < dimensionality; ++i) {
+    it.v_dimensions[i] = (r_ssize) v_dimensions[i];
+    it.v_strides[i] = v_strides[i];
+  }
+  it.dimensionality = rray__strided_iterator_axes_coalesce(
+    it.v_dimensions,
+    it.v_strides,
+    dimensionality
+  );
+
+  return it;
+}
+
+static inline struct rray_strided_iterator_v2 rray_broadcast_iterator_v2(
+  const int* v_from_dimensions,
+  int from_dimensionality,
+  const int* v_to_dimensions,
+  int to_dimensionality
+) {
+  check_max_dimensionality(to_dimensionality);
+
+  rray__check_broadcast_dimensions(
+    v_from_dimensions,
+    from_dimensionality,
+    v_to_dimensions,
+    to_dimensionality
+  );
+
+  r_ssize v_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
+
+  rray__fill_broadcast_strides(
+    v_from_dimensions,
+    from_dimensionality,
+    to_dimensionality,
+    v_strides
+  );
+
+  return rray_strided_iterator_v2(
+    v_to_dimensions,
+    to_dimensionality,
+    v_strides
+  );
+}
+
+static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_v2_run_size(
+  const struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it
+) {
+  return it->v_dimensions[0];
+}
+
+static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_v2_run_stride(
+  const struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it
+) {
+  return it->v_strides[0];
+}
+
+static inline bool rray_strided_iterator_v2_finished(
+  const struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it
+) {
+  return it->run_start == it->size;
+}
+
+static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_v2_run_start(
+  const struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it
+) {
+  return it->run_start;
+}
+
+static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_v2_location(
+  const struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it
+) {
+  return it->location;
+}
+
+static inline void rray_strided_iterator_v2_next(
+  struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it
+) {
+  it->run_start += rray_strided_iterator_v2_run_size(it);
+  for (int axis = 1; axis < it->dimensionality; ++axis) {
+    ++it->v_point[axis];
+    if (it->v_point[axis] < it->v_dimensions[axis]) {
+      it->location += it->v_strides[axis];
+      return;
+    }
+    it->v_point[axis] = 0;
+    it->location -= (it->v_dimensions[axis] - 1) * it->v_strides[axis];
+  }
+}
+
+struct rray_strided_iterator2_v2 {
+  r_ssize run_start;
+  r_ssize size;
+  r_ssize location1;
+  r_ssize location2;
+  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
+  r_ssize v_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
+  int dimensionality;
+  r_ssize v_strides1[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
+  r_ssize v_strides2[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
+};
+
+static inline struct rray_strided_iterator2_v2 rray_strided_iterator2_v2(
+  const int* v_dimensions,
+  int dimensionality,
+  const r_ssize* v_strides1,
+  const r_ssize* v_strides2
+) {
+  check_max_dimensionality(dimensionality);
+
+  struct rray_strided_iterator2_v2 it;
+
+  it.run_start = 0;
+  it.size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);
+  it.location1 = 0;
+  it.location2 = 0;
+  memset(it.v_point, 0, sizeof(r_ssize) * dimensionality);
+
+  for (int i = 0; i < dimensionality; ++i) {
+    it.v_dimensions[i] = (r_ssize) v_dimensions[i];
+    it.v_strides1[i] = v_strides1[i];
+    it.v_strides2[i] = v_strides2[i];
+  }
+  it.dimensionality = rray__strided_iterator_axes_coalesce2(
+    it.v_dimensions,
+    it.v_strides1,
+    it.v_strides2,
+    dimensionality
+  );
+
+  return it;
+}
+
+static inline struct rray_strided_iterator2_v2 rray_broadcast_iterator2_v2(
+  const int* v_from1_dimensions,
+  int from1_dimensionality,
+  const int* v_from2_dimensions,
+  int from2_dimensionality,
+  const int* v_to_dimensions,
+  int to_dimensionality
+) {
+  check_max_dimensionality(to_dimensionality);
+
+  rray__check_broadcast_dimensions(
+    v_from1_dimensions,
+    from1_dimensionality,
+    v_to_dimensions,
+    to_dimensionality
+  );
+
+  rray__check_broadcast_dimensions(
+    v_from2_dimensions,
+    from2_dimensionality,
+    v_to_dimensions,
+    to_dimensionality
+  );
+
+  r_ssize v_strides1[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
+  r_ssize v_strides2[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
+
+  rray__fill_broadcast_strides(
+    v_from1_dimensions,
+    from1_dimensionality,
+    to_dimensionality,
+    v_strides1
+  );
+
+  rray__fill_broadcast_strides(
+    v_from2_dimensions,
+    from2_dimensionality,

75 more lines cut

Thinking

Planning parallel benchmark runs

The first compiler result is encouraging but not cleanly free. The combined version keeps the hot loops vectorized, cuts the double-add worker stack frame from 0x260 to 0x70, and reduces object code size. It also emits stores of the two mutable locations back into the caller-owned struct between runs. Those stores are the exact cost the benchmark now needs to expose, especially for short first-axis runs.

Investigating vectorization impact on regression

Planning isolated multi-pass benchmarks

Thinking

Investigating vectorization impact on regression

Planning isolated multi-pass benchmarks

The first sequential timing pass is mixed. Equality and simple direct broadcast are flat or slightly faster, but several row-style arithmetic cases showed large slowdowns. Those medians are known to jump with allocation and garbage collection, so I’m rerunning the affected shapes in a focused suite with more iterations and balanced ordering before deciding whether the location stores are truly costly.

Thinking

Evaluating struct copy strategies for performance

The focused reruns now show the pattern clearly. Ordinary equality and row broadcasting are flat, but carry-heavy alternating shapes regress by roughly 15 to 18 percent. The assembly explains it: the values remain in registers, but Clang also synchronizes mutable locations to the caller-owned struct on each run. I’m leaving this direct combined-state form as the POC, since it tests the idea honestly, and I’m running the full suite now.

    17	  const int* v_dimensions,

   104	static inline void rray_strided_iterator_v2_next(
   105	  struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it
   106	) {
   107	  it->run_start += rray_strided_iterator_v2_run_size(it);
   108	  for (int axis = 1; axis < it->dimensionality; ++axis) {
   109	    ++it->v_point[axis];
   110	    if (it->v_point[axis] < it->v_dimensions[axis]) {
   111	      it->location += it->v_strides[axis];
   112	      return;
   113	    }
   114	    it->v_point[axis] = 0;
   115	    it->location -= (it->v_dimensions[axis] - 1) * it->v_strides[axis];
   116	  }
   117	}

Thinking

Deciding header naming and struct inclusion

Summarizing POC benchmarks and code changes

Implemented the combined-state POC in strided-iterator2.h.

Switched these paths to it:

  • Arithmetic through arithmetic.h
  • Equality in equal.c
  • Direct broadcasting in broadcast.c

The POC uses _v2 names, so other iterator users remain unchanged.

Performance findings, POC divided by current split implementation:

  • Alternating arithmetic: 1.18x
  • Alternating broadcast: 1.15x
  • Row arithmetic: 1.04x
  • Scalar arithmetic: 1.05x
  • Row equality: 0.98x
  • Row broadcast: 0.97x

The hot loops remain vectorized, and the double-add worker’s stack frame drops from 0x260 to 0x70. However, Clang stores the mutable locations back into the caller-owned struct between runs. The split implementation keeps those locations only in registers. This explains the regression in carry-heavy shapes.

Validation:

  • clang-format -i src/*.c src/*.h
  • air format .
  • Full tests: 1,512 passed
  • Protection review: no new r_obj* lifetime risks

The changes are uncommitted. The POC suggests the direct one-struct design is not performance-neutral, although its cost depends strongly on traversal shape.

Davis

“Clang stores the mutable locations back into the caller-owned struct between runs”

So if the struct was created inside the function that used it rather than being passed through a function pointer? Would that fix everything?

Codex

Thinking

Planning worker-local struct construction

Analyzing inlining effects on broadcast performance

Planning worker-local struct construction

Analyzing inlining effects on broadcast performance

Planning iterator construction inside worker with raw dimension args

Thinking

Planning worker-local struct construction

Analyzing inlining effects on broadcast performance

Planning iterator construction inside worker with raw dimension args

That should remove the function-pointer aliasing problem for arithmetic and equality, but it may not be the whole story. Direct broadcast already lets Clang inline the worker into the function that creates the iterator, yet the combined version still regressed on carry-heavy shapes. I’m testing a truly local combined object now to separate “crossed a function boundary” from “large mutable aggregate was not scalarized.”

Thinking

Analyzing stack size discrepancy

Examining constructor copy overhead

Thinking

Analyzing stack size discrepancy

Examining constructor copy overhead

Proposing in-place iterator initialization

It would fix the main arithmetic and equality problem, but not automatically everything.

I tested a worker-local combined iterator. Clang stopped writing location1 and location2 between runs. The focused alternating-add result improved from about 1.18x slower to 0.99x, effectively flat. Equality also remained flat.

That confirms the important property is ownership:

  • Passed mutable pointer: Clang keeps locations in registers but also synchronizes them to the caller’s struct.
  • Non-escaping local object: those stores disappear.

My temporary test copied the complete struct locally, so it was not a good final implementation. It added a 0x828-byte copy and increased the worker stack frame to 0x830. We should instead construct it directly, preferably with an in-place initializer:

struct rray_strided_iterator2_v2 it;

rray_broadcast_iterator2_v2_init(
  &it,
  v_x_dimensions,
  x_dimensionality,
  v_y_dimensions,
  y_dimensionality,
  v_dimensions,
  dimensionality
);

The difficulty is getting that dimension metadata across the type-dispatch function pointer without putting it in another struct. Arithmetic and equality workers would need several additional parameters.

Direct broadcast also shows that locality is not the only factor. Its worker is already inlined into the function that constructs the iterator, but the combined implementation still has a larger stack frame and its alternating case remains somewhat slower. Large-struct construction and temporary stride arrays affect that code too.

So:

  • Yes, local construction fixes the repeated mutable-location stores.
  • It likely makes arithmetic and equality competitive.
  • It does not guarantee the entire combined design is free.
  • The next useful POC is direct, in-place construction inside each typed worker, with raw dimension metadata passed through the dispatch boundary.

Davis

What does “in place construction” mean vs what is currently done?

Codex

Thinking

Explaining return-by-value versus in-place initialization

Currently, the constructor returns the whole struct by value:

struct rray_strided_iterator2_v2 it =
  rray_broadcast_iterator2_v2(...);

Inside rray_broadcast_iterator2_v2(), a local struct is created, filled, and returned:

struct rray_strided_iterator2_v2 it;

/* Fill it */

return it;

Clang will often remove the apparent copy, especially because the function is inline. With a large struct containing several fixed-size arrays, however, that optimization is not guaranteed.

“In-place construction” means the caller creates the storage and the initializer fills that exact object:

struct rray_strided_iterator2_v2 it;

rray_broadcast_iterator2_v2_init(
  &it,
  ...
);

The initializer would return void:

static inline void rray_broadcast_iterator2_v2_init(
  struct rray_strided_iterator2_v2* it,
  ...
) {
  it->run_start = 0;
  it->location1 = 0;
  it->location2 = 0;
  /* Fill the arrays */
}

This makes it explicit that there is only one struct:

Return by value: constructor temporary → returned value → caller variable
In place:        caller variable ← initializer writes directly

There are two separate concerns:

  • Local construction: Create the iterator inside the worker that traverses it. This helps Clang eliminate mutable-state stores.
  • In-place construction: Fill the worker’s local iterator through a pointer instead of returning a large struct. This avoids a possible large copy or temporary.

Because the constructors are inline, changing only to in-place initialization may produce identical assembly. It is mainly a way to make the desired storage behavior explicit and easier to verify.

Davis

Gotcha, okay try this then

“direct, in-place construction inside each typed worker, with raw dimension metadata passed through the dispatch boundary”

Codex

Thinking

Planning API changes and benchmarking

Redesigning arithmetic function signatures

Thinking

Planning API changes and benchmarking

Redesigning arithmetic function signatures

I’ll move iterator creation into each generated typed worker and change the constructors to fill caller-provided storage. The dispatch functions will pass only dimension pointers and dimensionalities. Then I’ll compare assembly and rerun the same focused benchmarks against the saved split baseline.

#ifndef RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR2_H
#define RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR2_H

#include "strided-iterator.h"

struct rray_strided_iterator_v2 {
  r_ssize run_start;
  r_ssize size;
  r_ssize location;
  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  r_ssize v_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  int dimensionality;
  r_ssize v_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
};

static inline void rray_strided_iterator_v2_init(
  struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it,
  const int* v_dimensions,
  int dimensionality,
  const r_ssize* v_strides
) {
  check_max_dimensionality(dimensionality);

  it->run_start = 0;
  it->size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);
  it->location = 0;
  memset(it->v_point, 0, sizeof(r_ssize) * dimensionality);

  for (int i = 0; i < dimensionality; ++i) {
    it->v_dimensions[i] = (r_ssize) v_dimensions[i];
    it->v_strides[i] = v_strides[i];
  }
  it->dimensionality = rray__strided_iterator_axes_coalesce(
    it->v_dimensions,
    it->v_strides,
    dimensionality
  );
}

static inline void rray_broadcast_iterator_v2_init(
  struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it,
  const int* v_from_dimensions,
  int from_dimensionality,
  const int* v_to_dimensions,
  int to_dimensionality
) {
  check_max_dimensionality(to_dimensionality);

  rray__check_broadcast_dimensions(
    v_from_dimensions,
    from_dimensionality,
    v_to_dimensions,
    to_dimensionality
  );

  r_ssize v_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];

  rray__fill_broadcast_strides(
    v_from_dimensions,
    from_dimensionality,
    to_dimensionality,
    v_strides
  );

  rray_strided_iterator_v2_init(
    it,
    v_to_dimensions,
    to_dimensionality,
    v_strides
  );
}

static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_v2_run_size(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it
) {
  return it->v_dimensions[0];
}

static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_v2_run_stride(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it
) {
  return it->v_strides[0];
}

static inline bool rray_strided_iterator_v2_finished(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it
) {
  return it->run_start == it->size;
}

static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_v2_run_start(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it
) {
  return it->run_start;
}

static inline r_ssize rray_strided_iterator_v2_location(
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it
) {
  return it->location;
}

static inline void rray_strided_iterator_v2_next(
  struct rray_strided_iterator_v2* it
) {
  it->run_start += rray_strided_iterator_v2_run_size(it);
  for (int axis = 1; axis < it->dimensionality; ++axis) {
    ++it->v_point[axis];
    if (it->v_point[axis] < it->v_dimensions[axis]) {
      it->location += it->v_strides[axis];
      return;
    }
    it->v_point[axis] = 0;
    it->location -= (it->v_dimensions[axis] - 1) * it->v_strides[axis];
  }
}

struct rray_strided_iterator2_v2 {
  r_ssize run_start;
  r_ssize size;
  r_ssize location1;
  r_ssize location2;
  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  r_ssize v_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  int dimensionality;
  r_ssize v_strides1[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  r_ssize v_strides2[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
};

static inline void rray_strided_iterator2_v2_init(
  struct rray_strided_iterator2_v2* it,
  const int* v_dimensions,
  int dimensionality,
  const r_ssize* v_strides1,
  const r_ssize* v_strides2
) {
  check_max_dimensionality(dimensionality);

  it->run_start = 0;
  it->size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);
  it->location1 = 0;
  it->location2 = 0;
  memset(it->v_point, 0, sizeof(r_ssize) * dimensionality);

  for (int i = 0; i < dimensionality; ++i) {
    it->v_dimensions[i] = (r_ssize) v_dimensions[i];
    it->v_strides1[i] = v_strides1[i];
    it->v_strides2[i] = v_strides2[i];
  }
  it->dimensionality = rray__strided_iterator_axes_coalesce2(
    it->v_dimensions,
    it->v_strides1,
    it->v_strides2,
    dimensionality
  );
}

    v_from1_dimensions,
    from1_dimensionality,
    to_dimensionality,
    v_strides1
  );

    v_from2_dimensions,
    from2_dimensionality,
    to_dimensionality,

Testing always_inline constructor attribute

Testing always_inline constructor attribute

The first result answers an important part: moving construction into the worker did not fix the regression. The carry-heavy cases are still about 19% slower than the split design, and Clang still spills the locations into the worker’s own stack object between runs. I’m checking whether this is only because it refused to inline the large initializer.

r_obj* rray_broadcast(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* dimensions,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  check_unclassed(x, arg, error_call);
  x = KEEP(arg_as_array(x, arg, error_call));

  dimensions =
    KEEP(arg_as_dimensions(dimensions, rray_args.dimensions, error_call));

  r_obj* x_dimensions = KEEP(rray_dimensions(x, arg, error_call));

  const int* v_x_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(x_dimensions);
  const int* v_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(dimensions);

  const int x_dimensionality =
    rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(x_dimensions);
  const int dimensionality = rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(dimensions);

  if (
    rray_dimensions_are_equal(
      v_x_dimensions,
      x_dimensionality,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality
    )
  ) {
    FREE(3);
    return x;
  }

  check_broadcastable(
    v_x_dimensions,
    x_dimensionality,
    v_dimensions,
    dimensionality,
    arg,
    error_call
  );

  const r_ssize size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);

  r_obj* out;

  switch (r_typeof(x)) {
  case R_TYPE_logical:
    out = rray_broadcast_lgl(
      x,
      size,
      v_x_dimensions,
      x_dimensionality,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality
    );
    break;
  case R_TYPE_integer:
    out = rray_broadcast_int(
      x,
      size,
      v_x_dimensions,
      x_dimensionality,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality
    );
    break;
  case R_TYPE_double:
    out = rray_broadcast_dbl(
      x,
      size,
      v_x_dimensions,
      x_dimensionality,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality
    );
    break;
  case R_TYPE_complex:
    out = rray_broadcast_cpl(
      x,
      size,
      v_x_dimensions,
      x_dimensionality,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality
    );
    break;
  case R_TYPE_raw:
    out = rray_broadcast_raw(
      x,
      size,
      v_x_dimensions,
      x_dimensionality,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality
    );
    break;
  case R_TYPE_character:
    out = rray_broadcast_chr(
      x,
      size,
      v_x_dimensions,
      x_dimensionality,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality
    );
    break;
  case R_TYPE_list:
    out = rray_broadcast_list(
      x,
      size,
      v_x_dimensions,
      x_dimensionality,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality
    );
    break;
  default:
    r_stop_unreachable();
  }

  KEEP(out);
  r_attrib_poke_dim(out, dimensions);

  r_obj* out_names = KEEP(rray_broadcast_names(x, dimensions));

  if (out_names != r_null) {
    r_attrib_poke_dim_names(out, out_names);
  }

  FREE(5);
  return out;
}

#define RRAY_BROADCAST_ATOMIC(RTYPE, CTYPE, CONST_DEREF, DEREF)                \

  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_vector(RTYPE, size));                              \

static r_obj* rray_equality(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* y,
  enum rray_equality_op op,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct rray_arg* y_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  check_unclassed(x, x_arg, error_call);
  check_unclassed(y, y_arg, error_call);

  y = KEEP(arg_as_array(y, y_arg, error_call));

    rray_equality_switch(x, y, op, x_arg, y_arg, error_call);

  r_obj* y_dimensions = r_dim(y);

  const int* v_y_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(y_dimensions);

  const int x_dimensionality =
    rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(x_dimensions);
  const int y_dimensionality =
    rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(y_dimensions);

  r_obj* dimensions = KEEP(rray_dimensions2(
    v_x_dimensions,
    x_dimensionality,
    v_y_dimensions,
    y_dimensionality,
    x_arg,
    y_arg,
    error_call
  ));
  const int* v_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(dimensions);
  const int dimensionality = rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(dimensions);

  const r_ssize size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);

  r_obj* out = KEEP(fn(
    x,
    y,
    size,
    v_x_dimensions,
    x_dimensionality,
    v_y_dimensions,

Thinking

Deciding on keeping POC despite regression

Analyzing inlining and scalar replacement limitations

Implemented the direct worker-local construction POC in strided-iterator2.h. Arithmetic, equality, and broadcast now pass raw dimension metadata through dispatch and construct the combined iterator inside each typed worker.

It did not recover the split design’s performance:

Case Change vs split
Add, alternating broadcast 19.3% slower
Broadcast, alternating 18.6% slower
Add, row 5.1% slower
Add, scalar 5.6% slower
Broadcast, row 1.3% slower
Equality, row 0.7% slower

These are geometric means from two 200-iteration runs.

The assembly explains why. The combined worker-local struct gets a 2,096-byte stack frame, compared with 608 bytes for the split version. Clang holds the locations in registers during each run, but writes them back into the local struct on every carry. Forcing the initializer to inline increased the frame to 3,168 bytes and did not remove those stores.

So “caller-owned” was too narrow. The important distinction is whether Clang can break the aggregate into independent values. The large combined, address-taken struct remains memory-backed even when it is local. Keeping the large immutable plan separate from the smaller mutable cursor gives Clang a much easier object to optimize.

All 1,512 tests pass. Formatting, diff checks, and the GC protection pass are clean.

Davis

Out of curiosity if we set max dimensionality to 8 what happens?

Codex

I’ll shrink the fixed arrays to eight axes, rebuild, inspect the worker stack frame and rerun the same focused comparison. This should tell us whether the combined struct is failing mainly because it is large.

Thinking

Preparing max8 baseline for benchmark

Thinking

Preparing max8 baseline for benchmark

With eight axes, the combined worker frame drops from 2,096 bytes to 416 bytes. That is smaller even than the 608-byte split worker at the old 64-axis limit. Clang still spills both locations on every carry, so now I’m measuring whether the smaller spills are cheap enough to erase the regression.

Bash 
const bench = `Rscript -e 'devtools::load_all(quiet = TRUE); n <- 1e6L; dimensions <- c(1000L, 1000L); six_dimensions <- rep(10L, 6L); x <- array(seq(1, 2, length.out = n), dimensions); scalar <- array(2, c(1L, 1L)); row <- array(seq(2, 3, length.out = 1000L), c(1L, 1000L)); six_x <- array(seq(1, 2, length.out = n), six_dimensions); six_inner <- array(seq(2, 3, length.out = 1000L), c(1L, 10L, 1L, 10L, 1L, 10L)); invisible(rray_add(x, row)); invisible(rray_add(x, scalar)); invisible(rray_add(six_x, six_inner)); invisible(rray_equal(x, row)); invisible(rray_broadcast(row, dimensions)); invisible(rray_broadcast(six_inner, six_dimensions)); mark <- bench::mark(add_row = rray_add(x, row), add_scalar = rray_add(x, scalar), add_alternating = rray_add(six_x, six_inner), equal_row = rray_equal(x, row), broadcast_row = rray_broadcast(row, dimensions), broadcast_alternating = rray_broadcast(six_inner, six_dimensions), iterations = 200, check = FALSE, memory = FALSE); out <- data.frame(case = as.character(mark$expression), median = as.numeric(mark$median), min = as.numeric(mark$min)); write.table(out, file = "/tmp/rray4-direct8-focused-1.tsv", sep = "\\t", row.names = FALSE, quote = FALSE)'`;

Thinking

Summarizing performance results and regressions

Reducing the maximum dimensionality to 8 shrinks the stack substantially, but does not fix the performance problem.

Worker Stack frame
Combined, 64 axes 2,096 bytes
Combined, 8 axes 416 bytes
Split, 8 axes 256 bytes

With both designs capped at eight axes, the combined version remained:

  • 18.5% slower for alternating arithmetic
  • 15.7% slower for alternating broadcast
  • Effectively equal for row, scalar, and equality cases

Clang still stores both mutable locations to the stack on every carry. Shrinking the arrays makes the frame cheaper, but does not remove that memory dependency. Since the frame already fits comfortably in L1 cache, its total size is not the main problem.

Setting the limit to eight also breaks tests covering the existing 64-axis contract. I restored the limit to 64, and all 1,512 tests pass again.

Davis

Okay, i think we’ve learned that we need to keep the smaller struct separate for best performance.

Remove all of the changes done so far, then in strided iterator.h after the opening paragraph of docs write 2-3 sentences about what we’ve learned, in simple english, and about why we have a two stage design

?? src/strided-iterator2.h
#ifndef RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR_H
#define RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR_H

#include "dimensionality.h"
#include "rlang.h"
#include "size.h"

#include "decl/strided-iterator-decl.h"

// Strided iterator
//
// Walks the multidimensional point space defined by `v_dimensions`. Reports a
// 1D `location` in an alternate subspace defined by `v_strides`.
//
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Examples
//
// For broadcasting, the dimensions you broadcast to make up the larger point
// space. This is walked in order. The original dimensions of the array make
// up the subspace. So as you walk the output's point space you can fetch
// `location`s back into your original array to pull from.
//
// For reducing, it's actually a special form of broadcasting. The original
// dimensions of the array are the point space. The reduced dimensions are the
// subspace. So as you walk the original array, you can fetch `location`s into
// the output to accumulate the reduced result at.
//
// For permuting axes, the permuted dimensions make up the point space. The
// original dimensions of the array make up the subspace. So as you walk the
// output's point space you can fetch `location`s back into your original array
// to pull from.
//
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Optimization - First axis runs
//
// After coalescing, the iterator walks the entire first axis in one inner run
// while holding all later axes fixed. It only updates the later point
// coordinates between runs, rather than checking and carrying them after every
// element. This gives the compiler a small loop where the index and locations
// advance by fixed strides, making it much easier to optimize and vectorize.
//
// Practical examples of when this is useful:
//
// - Identically shaped binary array operations. Adding two [2, 4, 5] arrays
//   coalesces to dimensions [40], so the entire operation is one first axis run
//   where both input locations advance contiguously.
//
// - Broadcasting over a later axis. Broadcasting a [2, 3] array to [2, 3, 4]
//   coalesces to dimensions [6, 4] with location strides [1, 0]. The iterator
//   performs four first axis runs of size 6, copying six contiguous values
//   before updating the later axis.
//
// - Reducing over a later axis. Reducing a [2, 3, 4] array over its third axis
//   also coalesces to dimensions [6, 4] with output strides [1, 0]. Each first
//   axis run accumulates one contiguous slice into six output locations before
//   advancing along the reduced axis.
//
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Optimization - Coalescing
//
// Coalescing axes is an important optimization used to reduce the number of
// axes we have to iterate over by "merging" adjacent compatible ones. This can
// improve performance by 3-5x on its own in some cases.
//
// Practical examples of when this is useful:
//
// - Identically shaped binary array operations. Adding two arrays with
//   dimensions [2, 4, 5] gives both inputs strides [1, 2, 8]. All adjacent axes
//   coalesce (2 * 1 = 2, 4 * 2 = 8) into one dimension [40] with stride [1],
//   resulting in one flat vectorizable loop.
//
// - Scalar broadcasting across an entire array. Adding a scalar to a [2, 4, 5]
//   array gives the array strides [1, 2, 8] and the scalar strides [0, 0, 0].
//   Both coalesce completely, producing a [40] loop with array stride [1] and
//   scalar stride [0].
//
// - Contiguous adjacent axes within a broadcast operation. Broadcasting a
//   [2, 3] array over point dimensions [2, 3, 4] produces location strides
//   [1, 2, 0]. The adjacent axes 1 and 2 coalesce (2 * 1 = 2) into size 6,
//   leaving dimensions [6, 4] and strides [1, 0].
//
// - Contiguous regions within a reduction. Reducing a [2, 3, 4] array over its
//   third axis maps into a [2, 3] result using location strides [1, 2, 0].
//   Coalescing produces dimensions [6, 4], making it the same as the broadcast
//   example above.
//
// - Point-space dimensions of size 1. Output point dimensions of [1, 3, 4] can
//   have strides [0, 1, 3]. The dimension 1 first axis is absorbed into the
//   dimension 3 second axis, adopting stride 1, after which the dimension 4
//   third axis also coalesces. The result is one dimension [12] with stride
//   [1]. Note that this isn't broadcasting. This is when both input and output
//   have an axis that stays dimension 1, which is somewhat rare.
//
// For iterator2, note that both sets of location strides must be coalescible,
// as coalescing changes the output dimensionality, so it's all or nothing.
//
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Optimization - Fixed zero stride paths
//
// After coalescing, the first axis is walked by the inner loop. A stride of 0
// on this axis means that a subspace location stays fixed while the point space
// moves along it. Broadcasting uses this to reuse an input value. Reducing uses
// it to accumulate into the same output location. A zero stride on a later axis
// does not use this path because later axes advance between inner runs.
//
// When the inner loop receives the stride as a runtime value, the compiler
// can't prove that the location is fixed and falls back to a scalar loop. The
// public iteration macros check for a zero stride and pass a literal 0 to a
// specialized path. This lets the compiler see that the location does not
// change. For binary operations, it can then hoist the fixed load out of the
// loop and vectorize the remaining stride 1 work.
//
// Practical examples of when this is useful:
//
// - Scalar broadcasting across an entire array. Adding a scalar to a [2, 4, 5]
//   array coalesces to point dimensions [40], with array strides [1] and scalar
//   strides [0]. The scalar location stays fixed while the array and output
//   advance contiguously. The same path is used by arithmetic, comparison,
//   equality, and extrema operations.
//
src/arithmetic-add.c
src/arithmetic-divide.c
src/arithmetic-exponentiate.c
src/arithmetic-multiply.c
src/arithmetic-subtract.c

src/decl/arithmetic-subtract-decl.h
src/decl/broadcast-decl.h
src/decl/equal-decl.h

@@
 // Walks the multidimensional point space defined by `v_dimensions`. Reports a
 // 1D `location` in an alternate subspace defined by `v_strides`.
 //
+// The iterator uses a two-stage design on purpose. The plan holds dimensions
+// and strides that do not change, while the iterator holds only the positions
+// that change as it walks. Keeping the changing state small lets the compiler
+// keep it in registers instead of repeatedly writing it to memory.
+//
 // --------------------------------------------------------------------------

 // Walks the multidimensional point space defined by `v_dimensions`. Reports a
 // 1D `location` in an alternate subspace defined by `v_strides`.
 //
+// The iterator uses a two-stage design on purpose. The plan holds dimensions
+// and strides that do not change, while the iterator holds only the positions
+// that change as it walks. Keeping the changing state small lets the compiler
+// keep it in registers instead of repeatedly writing it to memory.
